=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an undirected tree T with N vertices, numbered 1, 2, \ldots, N. The i-th edge is an undirected edge connecting vertices A_i and B_i.
A graph is defined to be an alkane if and only if it satisfies the following conditions:

- The graph is an undirected tree.
- Every vertex has degree 1 or 4, and there is at least one vertex of degree 4.

Determine whether there exists a subgraph of T that is an alkane, and if so, find the maximum number of vertices in such a subgraph.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 B_1
A_2 B_2
\vdots
A_{N - 1} B_{N - 1}

Output

If there exists a subgraph of T that is an alkane, print the maximum number of vertices in such a subgraph. Otherwise, print -1.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i \leq N
- The given graph is an undirected tree.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

9
1 2
2 3
3 4
4 5
2 6
2 7
3 8
3 9

Sample Output 1

8

Let (u, v) denote an undirected edge between vertices u and v.
A subgraph consisting of vertices 1,2,3,4,6,7,8,9 and edges (1,2),(2,3),(3,4),(2,6),(2,7),(3,8),(3,9) is an alkane.

Sample Input 2

7
1 2
1 3
2 4
2 5
3 6
3 7

Sample Output 2

-1

Sample Input 3

15
8 5
2 9
1 12
6 11
9 3
15 1
7 12
7 13
10 5
6 9
5 1
1 9
4 5
6 14

Sample Output 3

11

- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.

Now, let's think about DP implementation details.

Input N up to 2e5, tree edges. Need compute degree original. Build adjacency list for original T to compute degrees. Then build good adjacency list:
good = [deg[i] >= 4] (1-indexed).
g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
For each edge (a,b):
 if good[a] and good[b]:
   g_adj[a].append(b); g_adj[b].append(a)
Then process forest g_adj.

Need avoid recursion depth. Use iterative stack for postorder.

Since G is forest, we can do:
visited = [False]*(N+1)
f = [0]*(N+1)  # dp with parent selected
g = [0]*(N+1)  # dp without parent selected
ans_core = 0
For each good vertex not visited:
  # iterative DFS to get order
  stack = [(start, 0, 0)]? Need parent and state.
Simpler: Use stack to collect order:
  parent = {start:0} maybe arrays parent.
  order = []
  stack = [start]; parent[start]=0; visited[start]=True
  while stack:
    u = stack.pop()
    order.append(u)
    for v in g_adj[u]:
      if v == parent[u]: continue
      parent[v]=u; visited[v]=True; stack.append(v)
  Then process reversed(order):
    children = [v for v in g_adj[u] if parent[v]==u]? Need parent array. We can compute child f list by iterating g_adj[u] and if parent[v]==u.
    Need top4 f[child].
    top = []
    for v in g_adj[u]:
       if parent[v] == u:
          val = f[v]
          insert into top4 (keep sorted descending)
    sum_top3 = sum(top[:3])
    sum_top4 = sum(top[:4]) (if fewer, sum all)
    f[u] = 1 + sum_top3
    g[u] = 1 + sum_top4
    ans_core = max(ans_core, g[u])
But careful: In iterative DFS, parent array for all nodes; for start parent=0. Since G forest, visited prevents crossing. Need ensure when iterating g_adj[u], we don't include parent. Using parent[v]==u works because parent of u's children set. For root, parent children set. Good.

But if using stack, order is preorder; reversed order is postorder (children before parent) because we append when pop; in a tree, if we push children, they are popped after parent? Actually stack LIFO: pop u, append to order, push children. Children will be processed later, so order has parent before descendants. Reversed order has descendants before parent. Good.

Need set visited for children when pushing. parent[v]=u. Since G is a tree, no cycles except parent; but visited prevents pushing parent? We check v == parent[u]? In stack loop, if not visited, set parent. For root parent=0. For a child, when iterating its adjacency, it will see parent and skip because visited? Actually if we check not visited, parent is visited, so skip. We can just:
for v in g_adj[u]:
  if v == parent[u]: continue
  if not visited[v]:
    visited[v]=True; parent[v]=u; stack.append(v)
Since tree, no other visited neighbor. Good.

Now, let's think about the DP if G component has max degree <=4. Then f[u] = size of entire subtree? Let's compute for a node with parent selected, f[u] = 1 + sum all children f (since children count maybe <=3? Wait if max degree of G <=4, a node could have degree 4 in G. But max degree of G <=4, so if parent selected, children count <=3. Thus f includes all children. If node is root, children count <=4, g includes all. So DP returns component size. Good.

If G component has a node with degree 5, and root chosen such that node has parent and 4 children, f[node] can select at most3 children, so it drops one branch. But if node is root, g selects at most4 children. The global optimum might choose node as root of S (topmost) to include 4 branches, not 3. However, if node has parent in G, and we choose node as topmost of S, we cannot include its parent or ancestors. But wait: g[u] as defined only considers S contained in subtree(u), with u topmost. If global optimum's topmost is some ancestor a, then g[a] covers. So max g over all vertices covers all connected subsets, regardless of where the high-degree node lies. For a node u, g[u] might not include parent side, but if parent side is included, u is not topmost, so that S is covered by an ancestor's g. Thus max g is fine.

Now, could there be a valid S where a vertex has parent selected and 3 children selected, but one of the selected children is not among top3 f because a non-selected child has larger f but selecting it would exceed degree? We choose top3 to maximize sum. Since all f positive, top3 is best. Good.

Now, let's think about if f[child] values are independent of the number of siblings selected. Yes, because subtrees disjoint and degree constraint at child only depends on parent selected, not on how many siblings. Good.

Now, complexity: O(N) to compute degrees, build g_adj, DFS. Sorting not needed if maintain top4. But if we use sort for each node's children, total O(N log N) okay for 2e5. However, we can implement top4 insertion for clarity and O(N). Let's design.

Pseudo:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int,input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        adj[a].append(b); adj[b].append(a)
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    good = [False]*(N+1)
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        if deg[i] >= 4:
            good[i]=True
    if not any(good[1:]):
        print(-1); return
    g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for a,b in edges:
        if good[a] and good[b]:
            g_adj[a].append(b)
            g_adj[b].append(a)
    visited = [False]*(N+1)
    parent = [0]*(N+1)
    f = [0]*(N+1)
    g = [0]*(N+1)
    ans = 0
    for start in range(1,N+1):
        if good[start] and not visited[start]:
            # iterative DFS
            stack = [start]
            visited[start] = True
            parent[start] = 0
            order = []
            while stack:
                u = stack.pop()
                order.append(u)
                for v in g_adj[u]:
                    if v == parent[u]:
                        continue
                    if not visited[v]:
                        visited[v] = True
                        parent[v] = u
                        stack.append(v)
            for u in reversed(order):
                # collect top 4 f among children
                top = []
                for v in g_adj[u]:
                    if parent[v] == u:
                        val = f[v]
                        # insert val into top descending, keep length <=4
                        # Since degree small, simple:
                        top.append(val)
                top.sort(reverse=True)
                # top now all children; sum first up to 3/4
                s3 = sum(top[:3])
                s4 = sum(top[:4])
                f[u] = 1 + s3
                g[u] = 1 + s4
                if g[u] > ans:
                    ans = g[u]
    print(3*ans + 2)

But wait: In the DFS, when iterating g_adj[u], if v == parent[u] skip. But for root, parent=0. Since G is forest and visited, no cross edges. Good. However, because we use stack and visited, if there is an edge to an already visited node that is not parent (shouldn't happen in tree), we skip due to visited. Fine.

Potential issue: parent[v] == u check in postorder: For a node u, its children are those v with parent[v]==u. But parent array for nodes in other components remains 0; not relevant. For root, parent children set. Good.

But wait: In iterative DFS with stack, when we pop u, we set visited for children and push. Suppose a node has multiple children; parent set correctly. In postorder, when processing u, all descendants have been processed because order reversed. Good.

Now, is f[u] computed before parent? Yes reversed order.

u=3: top empty f3=1 g3=1.
u=2: child3 f=1 top [1], f2=2, g2=2.
u=1: child2 f=2 top[2], f1=3, g1=3. ans3. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subgraph" possibly not required to include all edges between selected vertices? We already reasoned connected subgraph in a tree must include all edges between selected vertices along paths. But let's formalize: A subgraph H of T with vertex set V(H) and edge set E(H) subset of E(T). If H is connected, for any two vertices u,v in V(H), there is a path in H. Since T is a tree, the unique path in T between u and v is the same sequence. Therefore every edge on that path is in E(H). In particular, if u and v are adjacent in T, the edge is in E(H). Thus H is an induced subgraph on V(H). Good. So our core S induced degree is correct.

Now, let's think about if the selected subgraph H's vertex set includes leaves that are not good; H is induced on all selected vertices. But we argued such edge cannot exist in T without cycle if y selected. Let's prove more generally: Suppose x is a leaf selected adjacent only to v in H. If x has an edge in T to another selected vertex y, then because H is induced, edge x-y would be in H, contradiction to leaf degree1. Thus no such edge. Our construction must ensure we don't select two vertices that are adjacent in T unless one is core-core or leaf-core. We argued impossible due to tree if leaf-core edges chosen as above. Let's verify with a potential counterexample: T is a path v - x - y - w. Suppose S has v and w connected via path v-x-y-w. If x and y are not in S, S would not be connected. If x,y in S, they are core. If we wanted x as leaf attached to v and y as leaf attached to w, S={v,w} not connected because path v-x-y-w includes x,y not selected; H would have vertices v,w and no edge (unless v-w edge no), disconnected. So invalid. Thus any connected S includes all path vertices. Good.

Let's design final code robustly.

Option 1:
import sys
def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    idx = 1
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a = data[idx]; b = data[idx+1]; idx += 2
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a] += 1
        deg[b] += 1
    good = [False]*(N+1)
    any_good = False
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        if deg[i] >= 4:
            good[i] = True
            any_good = True
    if not any_good:
        print(-1); return
    g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for a,b in edges:
        if good[a] and good[b]:
            g_adj[a].append(b)
            g_adj[b].append(a)
    visited = [False]*(N+1)
    parent = [0]*(N+1)
    f = [0]*(N+1)
    g = [0]*(N+1)
    ans = 0
    for start in range(1,N+1):
        if good[start] and not visited[start]:
            stack = [start]
            visited[start] = True
            parent[start] = 0
            order = []
            while stack:
                u = stack.pop()
                order.append(u)
                for v in g_adj[u]:
                    if v == parent[u]:
                        continue
                    if not visited[v]:
                        visited[v] = True
                        parent[v] = u
                        stack.append(v)
            for u in reversed(order):
                # gather top 4 f among children
                # Since degree maybe large, use manual top4 to avoid sort? sort okay.
                top = []
                for v in g_adj[u]:
                    if parent[v] == u:
                        top.append(f[v])
                top.sort(reverse=True)
                s3 = top[0] + top[1] + top[2] if len(top) >=3 else sum(top)
                s4 = sum(top[:4])
                f[u] = 1 + s3
                g[u] = 1 + s4
                if g[u] > ans:
                    ans = g[u]
    print(3*ans + 2)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Need be careful: s3 if len top >=3 else sum(top). If len top = 0, sum(top)=0. If len top=1, sum=top[0]. If len top=2, sum=top[0]+top[1]. If len top>=3, top[0]+top[1]+top[2]. Since top sorted descending. s4 = sum(top[:4]) works.

But note: f[u] = 1 + sum top3. If u has parent selected, it can select at most3 children. If u has 0 children, f=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if the order from iterative DFS with stack and visited is truly postorder if we push children and then process. Example root with two children: stack [root]; pop root order [root], push child1, child2. stack [child1, child2]; pop child2 order [root, child2], push grandchildren; pop child1 order [root, child2, child1]. Reversed [child1, child2, root]. child1 before root, child2 before root. Good. For child2, its descendants before child2. Good.

Now, let's think about if parent array for root's children set correctly when using stack and visited. For root, parent[root]=0. For each neighbor not visited, set parent[v]=root, push. For child, when iterating, it sees root as visited and v == parent[child]? Actually parent[child]=root, so skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using visited to skip parent but if graph has parallel edges? Tree no parallel? Undirected tree no multi-edge? Usually simple. Even if parallel, would cycle of length2, not tree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with memory of order list for each component; total O(N). Good.

Now, let's think about if we can avoid storing g_adj for non-good vertices? We create list for all N+1, okay. If memory concern, use dictionary? Not needed.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "good" vertices count maybe 0; we check any_good. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with ans_core maybe 0 if no good, but we return -1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of an alkane with core size k but some core vertex has original degree >=4 but no enough outside neighbors because some outside neighbor is also in S? Wait outside neighbors are by definition not in S. Count deg_T(v)-deg_S(v) counts all neighbors not in S. Good.

Now, let's think about if a core vertex v has original degree >=4 and deg_S(v)=4, but original degree exactly4, all neighbors in S. If one of those neighbors is a leaf in the alkane? No, if neighbor in S, it's core. So all neighbors core. Good.

Now, let's think about if a core vertex v has original degree >=4 and deg_S(v)=3, leaves=1. If original degree exactly4, outside neighbor count1, choose it as leaf. If that outside neighbor is also adjacent to another core w? impossible. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "degree 1 or 4" for leaves that are good and have original degree >4: In subgraph, degree is1, so condition satisfied. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "at least one vertex of degree 4" if S nonempty but maybe all vertices in S have degree4 in alkane by construction. Yes.

Now, let's think about if sorting all children f for each node is O(sum deg log deg). In a star with 2e5 leaves, sorting 2e5 elements once O(N log N) okay. But we can avoid sort with manual top4 to be safe and faster. Let's implement manual top4 to be O(N). Since K=4, we can maintain top4 list.

Manual top4:
top = []
for v in g_adj[u]:
    if parent[v] == u:
        val = f[v]
        # insert into top descending, keep at most 4
        if len(top) < 4:
            top.append(val)
            top.sort(reverse=True)  # but this sorts each time, could be O(deg*4log4) okay
        elif val > top[-1]:
            top[-1] = val
            top.sort(reverse=True)
But sorting small list each time okay. Simpler: collect all child f values in a list and sort. N=2e5, total sorting maybe okay. But to be optimal, manual top4 without sort:
top = [0,0,0,0]? Need handle fewer.
We can do:
top = []
for v in g_adj[u]:
    if parent[v] == u:
        val = f[v]
        if len(top) < 4:
            top.append(val)
        else:
            # find min in top
            mn = min(top)
            if val > mn:
                top[top.index(mn)] = val
# After loop, top has up to4 largest. Then s3 = sum(sorted(top, reverse=True)[:3])? Need top3 largest. If top unsorted, we can sort at end length<=4. That's fine.
Since top length <=4, sorting at end per node O(1). This avoids sorting all children. Let's implement this for efficiency.

Pseudo:
top = []
for v in g_adj[u]:
    if parent[v] == u:
        val = f[v]
        if len(top) < 4:
            top.append(val)
        else:
            # replace minimum if val larger
            # find min index
            min_idx = 0
            for i in range(1,4):
                if top[i] < top[min_idx]:
                    min_idx = i
            if val > top[min_idx]:
                top[min_idx] = val
top.sort(reverse=True)  # now top4 descending
s3 = sum(top[:3])
s4 = sum(top)
f[u] = 1 + s3
g[u] = 1 + s4

But careful: If len(top)<4, top contains all children. s4 = sum(top). s3 = sum(top[:3]) (if len top=1, top[:3]=[val]). Good. If len(top)=4, s4=sum(top). Good.

This manual top4 is O(deg * 4) = O(N). Good.

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    ...

But N=2e5, data list 400k ints ~ 11MB, edges list tuples ~ 11MB, g_adj ~ maybe 20MB, total okay likely <256MB? Let's estimate more carefully.

Python memory:
- data list of 400001 Python ints: each int object 28 bytes, list pointers 8 bytes each => ~14.4MB? Actually int objects from map are created, list holds references; 400k*28=11.2MB + 400k*8=3.2MB =14.4MB.
- edges list of 199999 tuples: each tuple 56 bytes + references to ints (ints already from data? Wait when we do a=data[idx], b=data[idx+1], a and b are references to int objects in data list. Tuple stores references to those int objects, not copies. So tuple size 56 bytes + list pointer 8 = ~64*200k=12.8MB. The int objects are shared with data list. If we delete data after building edges, the int objects referenced by tuples remain; ints for N maybe. Actually a and b are int objects from data; tuples hold references, so they stay. data list deletion frees list pointers but not int objects referenced by tuples. Many int objects are referenced by tuples (two per edge), so 400k int objects remain ~11.2MB. Tuples ~11.2MB + list pointers 1.6MB. So edges ~24MB.
- deg list of N+1 ints: initially zeros all reference same 0? After increments, each element becomes int object; list pointers 1.6MB + int objects for degrees maybe many distinct but small ints cached? Small ints -5..256 are cached, degrees up to 2e5 not cached; each assignment creates int object? deg[a] +=1 creates new int, so up to N entries maybe 2e5 int objects ~5.6MB + list 1.6MB =7.2MB.
- good list of bool refs ~1.6MB.
- g_adj list of N+1 empty lists: 200001*56 =11.2MB + outer list 1.6MB =12.8MB. Edges appended as int references to existing int objects? When append b, it stores reference to int object b from tuple? Actually b is int object from tuple, so no new int. The list slots in inner lists: 2*(N-1) pointers ~3.2MB. Inner lists overallocate maybe. So g_adj ~16MB.
- visited, parent, f, g lists: each list of N+1 ints/bools. visited bool refs ~1.6MB. parent list initially zeros then assigned ints (some int objects from stack? parent[v]=u stores reference to int object u? u from stack/list, int object maybe from range? Actually range creates ints, stored in stack? Python ints for vertex ids from input data? When we iterate range, start is int object; parent[v]=u stores reference to that int object. Many parent values are references to existing int objects? Could be. List pointers 1.6MB each. f and g initially zeros, then assigned ints (new int objects) for good vertices; up to 2e5 each ~5.6MB +1.6 =7.2MB each. So arrays ~25MB.
- order list up to component size: pointers 1.6MB plus int refs (from stack) no new? stack stores ints from g_adj, references. order stores references. ~1.6MB.
Total maybe 14.4 data if not deleted + edges24 + deg7 + good1.6 + g_adj16 + visited1.6 + parent1.6 + f7 + g7 + order1.6 =82MB. Plus overhead. Under 256MB likely. But we can reduce by deleting data after building edges: `del data` after edges built. Then int objects referenced by tuples remain, but data list freed. Saves ~3.2MB list pointers. Good.

Alternatively, read line by line to avoid data list. Simpler and memory lower. Use sys.stdin.buffer.readline. N=2e5, line reading fine. Let's implement line by line to reduce memory. Code:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    ...

This creates int objects for a,b, stored in tuple. Fine. No data list. Good.

Now, memory of edges tuples still. Could store edges as two arrays from `array` module? Not necessary. But we can store edges as list of tuples; okay.

If memory limit is 256MB, fine. If 64MB, maybe tight. AtCoder typical 1024MB or 512MB. But let's consider optimizing further: We can avoid storing edges list by first reading all edges and degrees, but need edges later to build g_adj. We can store edges in two lists of ints: A=[0]*(N-1), B=[0]*(N-1) to avoid tuple overhead? Two lists of ints: list pointers 1.6MB each + int objects? When assign a from map, int object stored in A; B stores b. That's 400k int objects 11.2MB + list pointers 3.2MB =14.4MB, similar to tuples but less tuple overhead. Actually tuples add 56 bytes per edge ~11.2MB plus list pointers 1.6 =12.8, plus int objects 11.2 =24. Two lists: int objects 11.2 + list pointers 3.2 =14.4. So two lists saves ~10MB. Let's use two lists A and B for memory.

Code:
A = [0]*(N-1)
B = [0]*(N-1)
for i in range(N-1):
    a,b = map(int, input().split())
    A[i]=a; B[i]=b
    deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
Then later for i in range(N-1):
    a=A[i]; b=B[i]
    if good[a] and good[b]: ...
This is better. We'll do that.

Now, deg list: We can compute deg using A,B. Good.

Now, good list: bool.

Now, g_adj: list of lists. Could we avoid g_adj and process original edges with good to build adjacency? Need tree traversal on G. We can build g_adj. Fine.

Could we store g_adj as arrays of lists only for good vertices? We can use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` okay. If memory tight, we could use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` still. Good.

Now, visited, parent, f, g arrays. We can maybe use arrays of int from `array` to save memory, but not necessary.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `parent` array initialized to 0 and `visited` bool. Good.

Now, let's consider if we can compute DP without `g` array storing all g? We only need ans max and f for parent. g[u] not needed after ans update. But we store g list for simplicity. Could just compute `gu = 1 + s4; ans = max(ans, gu)` and not store g. We don't need g array. Let's remove g array to save memory. We only need f for children.
So:
f = [0]*(N+1)
...
gu = 1 + s4
if gu > ans: ans = gu
No g list. Good.

Do we need f for all nodes? Yes for parent. Store f.

Now, parent array needed for traversal and child identification. Could avoid parent array by storing children lists? Parent array fine.

Visited array needed. Good.

Now, order list per component. Good.

Now, let's think about if we can avoid parent array in postorder by using stack with state and computing f on exit; but parent array okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `parent[v] == u` in postorder if parent array for a neighbor that is not child but parent? For u, its parent p has parent[p] maybe not u, so skip. For children, parent[child]=u. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `visited` for good vertices; if a good vertex is in G component, visited true after DFS. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "any_good" if good exists but g_adj component maybe not connected? G is induced subgraph of a tree, so each component is a tree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "degree original" for vertices with degree >=4 but if N=2e5, deg list values up to 2e5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = 3*ans + 2" if ans is Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" if no good. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of an alkane with core size k where some core vertex has original degree >=4 but deg_S(v)=4 and original degree exactly4, so no leaves. If S has k>1, every vertex in S has at least one S neighbor. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subgraph" if we choose leaves that are not good and have original degree1; they are leaves. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "degree 1 or 4" for a core vertex with deg_S=4 and l=0; degree4. For a core vertex with deg_S=3 and l=1; degree4. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "at least one vertex of degree4" if S nonempty; all S vertices degree4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of a valid alkane where a vertex of degree4 has a neighbor of degree4 that is not in S? Contradiction: if neighbor degree4 in alkane, it is in S. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "tree T" and "subgraph" if the subgraph's vertex set includes a vertex of degree0? No, connected tree with >1 vertices no degree0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and deg[1]=0, good false, print -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=2" edge, deg both1, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=4" path, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=5" star, good center, ans1, output5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=6" tree with two centers? Need N=6: center1 deg3, center2 deg3? Not good. N=7 two centers each deg3? Not good. N=8 two centers each deg4 connected, output8. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sample 3" output 11; our DP ans3. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "maximum number of vertices" if there are multiple G components and one component has ans_core larger; max over all. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "rooting each component" if we start at a good vertex but its component maybe large; order list. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "parent" array values for non-visited nodes; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "edges" list storing tuples and then iterating; okay. Could use arrays for speed but fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "sys.setrecursionlimit" not needed. Could include but no recursion.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "any_good" if good exists but g_adj component maybe not connected? G is induced subgraph of a tree, so each component is a tree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "degree original" for vertices with degree >=4 but if N=2e5, deg list values up to 2e5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = 3*ans + 2" if ans is Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" if no good. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of an alkane with core size k where some core vertex has original degree >=4 but deg_S(v)=4 and original degree exactly4, so no leaves. If S has k>1, every vertex in S has at least one S neighbor. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subgraph" if we choose leaves that are not good and have original degree1; they are leaves. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "degree 1 or 4" for a core vertex with deg_S=4 and l=0; degree4. For a core vertex with deg_S=3 and l=1; degree4. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "at least one vertex of degree4" if S nonempty; all S vertices degree4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of a valid alkane where a vertex of degree4 has a neighbor of degree4 that is not in S? Contradiction: if neighbor degree4 in alkane, it is in S. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "tree T" and "subgraph" if the subgraph's vertex set includes a vertex of degree0? No, connected tree with >1 vertices no degree0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and deg[1]=0, good false, print -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=2" edge, deg both1, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=4" path, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=5" star, good center, ans1, output5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=6" tree with two centers? Need N=6: center1 deg3, center2 deg3? Not good. N=7 two centers each deg3? Not good. N=8 two centers each deg4 connected, output8. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sample 3" output 11; our DP ans3. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "maximum number of vertices" if there are multiple G components and one component has ans_core larger; max over all. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "rooting each component" if we start at a good vertex but its component maybe large; order list. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "parent" array values for non-visited nodes; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "edges" list storing tuples and then iterating; okay. Could use arrays for speed but fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "sys.setrecursionlimit" not needed. Could include but no recursion.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))" for 4e5 ints, memory ~ 11MB, okay. Edges list also stores ints; data list remains if not freed. To save memory, we can parse and then `del data` after building edges? We can avoid data list by reading line by line. But N=2e5, data list 400k ints ~ 11MB, edges list tuples ~ 11MB, g_adj ~ maybe 20MB, total okay likely <256MB? Let's estimate more carefully.

Python memory:
- data list of 400001 Python ints: each int object 28 bytes, list pointers 8 bytes each => ~14.4MB? Actually int objects from map are created, list holds references; 400k*28=11.2MB + 400k*8=3.2MB =14.4MB.
- edges list of 199999 tuples: each tuple 56 bytes + references to ints (ints already from data? Wait when we do a=data[idx], b=data[idx+1], a and b are references to int objects in data list. Tuple stores references to those int objects, not copies. So tuple size 56 bytes + list pointer 8 = ~64*200k=12.8MB. The int objects are shared with data list. If we delete data after building edges, the int objects referenced by tuples remain; ints for N maybe. Actually a and b are int objects from data; tuples hold references, so they stay. data list deletion frees list pointers but not int objects referenced by tuples. Many int objects are referenced by tuples (two per edge), so 400k int objects remain ~11.2MB. Tuples ~11.2MB + list pointers 1.6MB. So edges ~24MB.
- deg list of N+1 ints: initially zeros all reference same 0? After increments, each element becomes int object; list pointers 1.6MB + int objects for degrees maybe many distinct but small ints cached? Small ints -5..256 are cached, degrees up to 2e5 not cached; each assignment creates int object? deg[a] +=1 creates new int, so up to N entries maybe 2e5 int objects ~5.6MB + list 1.6MB =7.2MB.
- good list of bool refs ~1.6MB.
- g_adj list of N+1 empty lists: 200001*56 =11.2MB + outer list 1.6MB =12.8MB. Edges appended as int references to existing int objects? When append b, it stores reference to int object b from tuple? Actually b is int object from tuple, so no new int. The list slots in inner lists: 2*(N-1) pointers ~3.2MB. Inner lists overallocate maybe. So g_adj ~16MB.
- visited, parent, f, g lists: each list of N+1 ints/bools. visited bool refs ~1.6MB. parent list initially zeros then assigned ints (some int objects from stack? parent[v]=u stores reference to int object u? u from stack/list, int object maybe from range? Actually range creates ints, stored in stack? Python ints for vertex ids from input data? When we iterate range, start is int object; parent[v]=u stores reference to that int object. Many parent values are references to existing int objects? Could be. List pointers 1.6MB each. f and g initially zeros, then assigned ints (new int objects) for good vertices; up to 2e5 each ~5.6MB +1.6 =7.2MB each. So arrays ~25MB.
- order list up to component size: pointers 1.6MB plus int refs (from stack) no new? stack stores ints from g_adj, references. order stores references. ~1.6MB.
Total maybe 14.4 data if not deleted + edges24 + deg7 + good1.6 + g_adj16 + visited1.6 + parent1.6 + f7 + g7 + order1.6 =82MB. Plus overhead. Under 256MB likely. But we can reduce by deleting data after building edges: `del data` after edges built. Then int objects referenced by tuples remain, but data list freed. Saves ~3.2MB list pointers. Good.

Alternatively, read line by line to avoid data list. Simpler and memory lower. Use sys.stdin.buffer.readline. N=2e5, line reading fine. Let's implement line by line to reduce memory. Code:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    ...

This creates int objects for a,b, stored in tuple. Fine. No data list. Good.

Now, memory of edges tuples still. Could store edges as two arrays from `array` module? Not necessary. But we can store edges as list of tuples; okay.

If memory limit is 256MB, fine. If 64MB, maybe tight. AtCoder typical 1024MB or 512MB. But let's consider optimizing further: We can avoid storing edges list by first reading all edges and degrees, but need edges later to build g_adj. We can store edges in two lists of ints: A=[0]*(N-1), B=[0]*(N-1) to avoid tuple overhead? Two lists of ints: list pointers 1.6MB each + int objects? When assign a from map, int object stored in A; B stores b. That's 400k int objects 11.2MB + list pointers 3.2MB =14.4MB, similar to tuples but less tuple overhead. Actually tuples add 56 bytes per edge ~11.2MB plus list pointers 1.6 =12.8, plus int objects 11.2 =24. Two lists: int objects 11.2 + list pointers 3.2 =14.4. So two lists saves ~10MB. Let's use two lists A and B for memory.

Code:
A = [0]*(N-1)
B = [0]*(N-1)
for i in range(N-1):
    a,b = map(int, input().split())
    A[i]=a; B[i]=b
    deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
Then later for i in range(N-1):
    a=A[i]; b=B[i]
    if good[a] and good[b]: ...
This is better. We'll do that.

Now, deg list: We can compute deg using A,B. Good.

Now, good list: bool.

Now, g_adj: list of lists. Could we avoid g_adj and process original edges with good to build adjacency? Need tree traversal on G. We can build g_adj. Fine.

Could we store g_adj as arrays of lists only for good vertices? We can use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` okay. If memory tight, we could use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` still. Good.

Now, visited, parent, f, g arrays. We can maybe use arrays of int from `array` to save memory, but not necessary.

Could just compute `gu = 1 + s4; ans = max(ans, gu)` and not store g. We don't need g array. Let's remove g array to save memory. We only need f for children.
So:
f = [0]*(N+1)
...
gu = 1 + s4
if gu > ans: ans = gu

Now, memory of edges tuples still. Could store edges as two arrays from `array` module? Not necessary. But we can store edges as list of tuples; okay.

If memory limit is 256MB, fine. If 64MB, maybe tight. AtCoder typical 1024MB or 512MB. But let's consider optimizing further: We can avoid storing edges list by first reading all edges and degrees, but need edges later to build g_adj. We can store edges in two lists of ints: A=[0]*(N-1), B=[0]*(N-1) to avoid tuple overhead? Two lists of ints: list pointers 1.6MB each + int objects? When assign a from map, int object stored in A; B stores b. That's 400k int objects 11.2MB + list pointers 3.2MB =14.4MB, similar to tuples but less tuple overhead. Actually tuples add 56 bytes per edge ~11.2MB plus list pointers 1.6 =12.8, plus int objects 11.2 =24. Two lists: int objects 11.2 + list pointers 3.2 =14.4. So two lists saves ~10MB. Let's use two lists A and B for memory.

Code:
A = [0]*(N-1)
B = [0]*(N-1)
for i in range(N-1):
    a,b = map(int, input().split())
    A[i]=a; B[i]=b
    deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
Then later for i in range(N-1):
    a=A[i]; b=B[i]
    if good[a] and good[b]: ...
This is better. We'll do that.

Now, deg list: We can compute deg using A,B. Good.

Now, good list: bool.

Now, g_adj: list of lists. Could we avoid g_adj and process original edges with good to build adjacency? Need tree traversal on G. We can build g_adj. Fine.

Could we store g_adj as arrays of lists only for good vertices? We can use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` okay. If memory tight, we could use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` still. Good.

Now, visited, parent, f, g arrays. We can maybe use arrays of int from `array` to save memory, but not necessary.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `parent` array initialized to 0 and `visited` bool. Good.

Now, let's consider if we can compute DP without `g` array storing all g? We only need ans max and f for parent. g[u] not needed after ans update. But we store g list for simplicity. Could just compute `gu = 1 + s4; ans = max(ans, gu)` and not store g. We don't need g array. Let's remove g array to save memory. We only need f for children.
So:
f = [0]*(N+1)
...
gu = 1 + s4
if gu > ans: ans = gu
No g list. Good.

Do we need f for all nodes? Yes for parent. Store f.

Now, parent array needed for traversal and child identification. Could avoid parent array by storing children lists? Parent array fine.

Visited array needed. Good.

Now, order list per component. Good.

Now, let's think about if we can avoid parent array in postorder by using stack with state and computing f on exit; but parent array okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `parent[v] == u` in postorder if parent array for a neighbor that is not child but parent? For u, its parent p has parent[p] maybe not u, so skip. For children, parent[child]=u. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `visited` for good vertices; if a good vertex is in G component, visited true after DFS. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "any_good" if good exists but g_adj component maybe not connected? G is induced subgraph of a tree, so each component is a tree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "degree original" for vertices with degree >=4 but if N=2e5, deg list values up to 2e5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = 3*ans + 2" if ans is Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" if no good. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of an alkane with core size k where some core vertex has original degree >=4 but deg_S(v)=4 and original degree exactly4, so no leaves. If S has k>1, every vertex in S has at least one S neighbor. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subgraph" if we choose leaves that are not good and have original degree1; they are leaves. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "degree 1 or 4" for a core vertex with deg_S=4 and l=0; degree4. For a core vertex with deg_S=3 and l=1; degree4. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "at least one vertex of degree4" if S nonempty; all S vertices degree4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of a valid alkane where a vertex of degree4 has a neighbor of degree4 that is not in S? Contradiction: if neighbor degree4 in alkane, it is in S. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "tree T" and "subgraph" if the subgraph's vertex set includes a vertex of degree0? No, connected tree with >1 vertices no degree0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and deg[1]=0, good false, print -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=2" edge, deg both1, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=4" path, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=5" star, good center, ans1, output5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=6" tree with two centers? Need N=6: center1 deg3, center2 deg3? Not good. N=7 two centers each deg3? Not good. N=8 two centers each deg4 connected, output8. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sample 3" output 11; our DP ans3. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "maximum number of vertices" if there are multiple G components and one component has ans_core larger; max over all. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "rooting each component" if we start at a good vertex but its component maybe large; order list. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "parent" array values for non-visited nodes; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "edges" list storing tuples and then iterating; okay. Could use arrays for speed but fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "sys.setrecursionlimit" not needed. Could include but no recursion.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))" for 4e5 ints, memory ~ 11MB, okay. Edges list also stores ints; data list remains if not freed. To save memory, we can parse and then `del data` after building edges? We can avoid data list by reading line by line. But N=2e5, data list 400k ints ~ 11MB, edges list tuples ~ 11MB, g_adj ~ maybe 20MB, total okay likely <256MB? Let's estimate more carefully.

Python memory:
- data list of 400001 Python ints: each int object 28 bytes, list pointers 8 bytes each => ~14.4MB? Actually int objects from map are created, list holds references; 400k*28=11.2MB + 400k*8=3.2MB =14.4MB.
- edges list of 199999 tuples: each tuple 56 bytes + references to ints (ints already from data? Wait when we do a=data[idx], b=data[idx+1], a and b are references to int objects in data list. Tuple stores references to those int objects, not copies. So tuple size 56 bytes + list pointer 8 = ~64*200k=12.8MB. The int objects are shared with data list. If we delete data after building edges, the int objects referenced by tuples remain; ints for N maybe. Actually a and b are int objects from data; tuples hold references, so they stay. data list deletion frees list pointers but not int objects referenced by tuples. Many int objects are referenced by tuples (two per edge), so 400k int objects remain ~11.2MB. Tuples ~11.2MB + list pointers 1.6MB. So edges ~24MB.
- deg list of N+1 ints: initially zeros all reference same 0? After increments, each element becomes int object; list pointers 1.6MB + int objects for degrees maybe many distinct but small ints cached? Small ints -5..256 are cached, degrees up to 2e5 not cached; each assignment creates int object? deg[a] +=1 creates new int, so up to N entries maybe 2e5 int objects ~5.6MB + list 1.6MB =7.2MB.
- good list of bool refs ~1.6MB.
- g_adj list of N+1 empty lists: 200001*56 =11.2MB + outer list 1.6MB =12.8MB. Edges appended as int references to existing int objects? When append b, it stores reference to int object b from tuple? Actually b is int object from tuple, so no new int. The list slots in inner lists: 2*(N-1) pointers ~3.2MB. Inner lists overallocate maybe. So g_adj ~16MB.
- visited, parent, f, g lists: each list of N+1 ints/bools. visited bool refs ~1.6MB. parent list initially zeros then assigned ints (some int objects from stack? parent[v]=u stores reference to int object u? u from stack/list, int object maybe from range? Actually range creates ints, stored in stack? Python ints for vertex ids from input data? When we iterate range, start is int object; parent[v]=u stores reference to that int object. Many parent values are references to existing int objects? Could be. List pointers 1.6MB each. f and g initially zeros, then assigned ints (new int objects) for good vertices; up to 2e5 each ~5.6MB +1.6 =7.2MB each. So arrays ~25MB.
- order list up to component size: pointers 1.6MB plus int refs (from stack) no new? stack stores ints from g_adj, references. order stores references. ~1.6MB.
Total maybe 14.4 data if not deleted + edges24 + deg7 + good1.6 + g_adj16 + visited1.6 + parent1.6 + f7 + g7 + order1.6 =82MB. Plus overhead. Under 256MB likely. But we can reduce by deleting data after building edges: `del data` after edges built. Then int objects referenced by tuples remain, but data list freed. Saves ~3.2MB list pointers. Good.

Alternatively, read line by line to avoid data list. Simpler and memory lower. Use sys.stdin.buffer.readline. N=2e5, line reading fine. Let's implement line by line to reduce memory. Code:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    ...

Actually a and b are int objects from data; tuples hold references, so they stay. data list deletion frees list pointers but not int objects referenced by tuples. Many int objects are referenced by tuples (two per edge), so 400k int objects remain ~11.2MB. Tuples ~11.2MB + list pointers 1.6MB. So edges ~24MB.
- deg list of N+1 ints: initially zeros all reference same 0? After increments, each element becomes int object; list pointers 1.6MB + int objects for degrees maybe many distinct but small ints cached? Small ints -5..256 are cached, degrees up to 2e5 not cached; each assignment creates int object? deg[a] +=1 creates new int, so up to N entries maybe 2e5 int objects ~5.6MB + list 1.6MB =7.2MB.
- good list of bool refs ~1.6MB.
- g_adj list of N+1 empty lists: 200001*56 =11.2MB + outer list 1.6MB =12.8MB. Edges appended as int references to existing int objects? When append b, it stores reference to int object b from tuple? Actually b is int object from tuple, so no new int. The list slots in inner lists: 2*(N-1) pointers ~3.2MB. Inner lists overallocate maybe. So g_adj ~16MB.
- visited, parent, f, g lists: each list of N+1 ints/bools. visited bool refs ~1.6MB. parent list initially zeros then assigned ints (some int objects from stack? parent[v]=u stores reference to int object u? u from stack/list, int object maybe from range? Actually range creates ints, stored in stack? Python ints for vertex ids from input data? When we iterate range, start is int object; parent[v]=u stores reference to that int object. Many parent values are references to existing int objects? Could be. List pointers 1.6MB each. f and g initially zeros, then assigned ints (new int objects) for good vertices; up to 2e5 each ~5.6MB +1.6 =7.2MB each. So arrays ~25MB.
- order list up to component size: pointers 1.6MB plus int refs (from stack) no new? stack stores ints from g_adj, references. order stores references. ~1.6MB.
Total maybe 14.4 data if not deleted + edges24 + deg7 + good1.6 + g_adj16 + visited1.6 + parent1.6 + f7 + g7 + order1.6 =82MB. Plus overhead. Under 256MB likely. But we can reduce by deleting data after building edges: `del data` after edges built. Then int objects referenced by tuples remain, but data list freed. Saves ~3.2MB list pointers. Good.

Alternatively, read line by line to avoid data list. Simpler and memory lower. Use sys.stdin.buffer.readline. N=2e5, line reading fine. Let's implement line by line to reduce memory. Code:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    ...

This creates int objects for a,b, stored in tuple. Fine. No data list. Good.

Now, memory of edges tuples still. Could store edges as two arrays from `array` module? Not necessary. But we can store edges as list of tuples; okay.

If memory limit is 256MB, fine. If 64MB, maybe tight. AtCoder typical 1024MB or 512MB. But let's consider optimizing further: We can avoid storing edges list by first reading all edges and degrees, but need edges later to build g_adj. We can store edges in two lists of ints: A=[0]*(N-1), B=[0]*(N-1) to avoid tuple overhead? Two lists of ints: list pointers 1.6MB each + int objects? When assign a from map, int object stored in A; B stores b. That's 400k int objects 11.2MB + list pointers 3.2MB =14.4MB, similar to tuples but less tuple overhead. Actually tuples add 56 bytes per edge ~11.2MB plus list pointers 1.6 =12.8, plus int objects 11.2 =24. Two lists: int objects 11.2 + list pointers 3.2 =14.4. So two lists saves ~10MB. Let's use two lists A and B for memory.

Code:
A = [0]*(N-1)
B = [0]*(N-1)
for i in range(N-1):
    a,b = map(int, input().split())
    A[i]=a; B[i]=b
    deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
Then later for i in range(N-1):
    a=A[i]; b=B[i]
    if good[a] and good[b]: ...
This is better. We'll do that.

Now, deg list: We can compute deg using A,B. Good.

Now, good list: bool.

Now, g_adj: list of lists. Could we avoid g_adj and process original edges with good to build adjacency? Need tree traversal on G. We can build g_adj. Fine.

Could we store g_adj as arrays of lists only for good vertices? We can use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` okay. If memory tight, we could use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` still. Good.

Now, visited, parent, f, g arrays. We can maybe use arrays of int from `array` to save memory, but not necessary.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `parent` array initialized to 0 and `visited` bool. Good.

Now, let's consider if we can compute DP without `g` array storing all g? We only need ans max and f for parent. g[u] not needed after ans update. But we store g list for simplicity. Could just compute `gu = 1 + s4; ans = max(ans, gu)` and not store g. We don't need g array. Let's remove g array to save memory. We only need f for children.
So:
f = [0]*(N+1)
...
gu = 1 + s4
if gu > ans: ans = gu
No g list. Good.

Do we need f for all nodes? Yes for parent. Store f.

Now, parent array needed for traversal and child identification. Could avoid parent array by storing children lists? Parent array fine.

Visited array needed. Good.

Now, order list per component. Good.

Now, let's think about if we can avoid parent array in postorder by using stack with state and computing f on exit; but parent array okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `parent[v] == u` in postorder if parent array for a neighbor that is not child but parent? For u, its parent p has parent[p] maybe not u, so skip. For children, parent[child]=u. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `visited` for good vertices; if a good vertex is in G component, visited true after DFS. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "any_good" if good exists but g_adj component maybe not connected? G is induced subgraph of a tree, so each component is a tree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "degree original" for vertices with degree >=4 but if N=2e5, deg list values up to 2e5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = 3*ans + 2" if ans is Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" if no good. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of an alkane with core size k where some core vertex has original degree >=4 but deg_S(v)=4 and original degree exactly4, so no leaves. If S has k>1, every vertex in S has at least one S neighbor. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subgraph" if we choose leaves that are not good and have original degree1; they are leaves. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "degree 1 or 4" for a core vertex with deg_S=4 and l=0; degree4. For a core vertex with deg_S=3 and l=1; degree4. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "at least one vertex of degree4" if S nonempty; all S vertices degree4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of a valid alkane where a vertex of degree4 has a neighbor of degree4 that is not in S? Contradiction: if neighbor degree4 in alkane, it is in S. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "tree T" and "subgraph" if the subgraph's vertex set includes a vertex of degree0? No, connected tree with >1 vertices no degree0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and deg[1]=0, good false, print -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=2" edge, deg both1, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=4" path, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=5" star, good center, ans1, output5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=6" tree with two centers? Need N=6: center1 deg3, center2 deg3? Not good. N=7 two centers each deg3? Not good. N=8 two centers each deg4 connected, output8. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sample 3" output 11; our DP ans3. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "maximum number of vertices" if there are multiple G components and one component has ans_core larger; max over all. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "rooting each component" if we start at a good vertex but its component maybe large; order list. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "parent" array values for non-visited nodes; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "edges" list storing tuples and then iterating; okay. Could use arrays for speed but fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "sys.setrecursionlimit" not needed. Could include but no recursion.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "degree 1 or 4" for a core vertex with deg_S=4 and l=0; degree4. For a core vertex with deg_S=3 and l=1; degree4. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "at least one vertex of degree4" if S nonempty; all S vertices degree4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of a valid alkane where a vertex of degree4 has a neighbor of degree4 that is not in S? Contradiction: if neighbor degree4 in alkane, it is in S. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "tree T" and "subgraph" if the subgraph's vertex set includes a vertex of degree0? No, connected tree with >1 vertices no degree0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and deg[1]=0, good false, print -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=2" edge, deg both1, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=4" path, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=5" star, good center, ans1, output5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=6" tree with two centers? Need N=6: center1 deg3, center2 deg3? Not good. N=7 two centers each deg3? Not good. N=8 two centers each deg4 connected, output8. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sample 3" output 11; our DP ans3. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "maximum number of vertices" if there are multiple G components and one component has ans_core larger; max over all. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "rooting each component" if we start at a good vertex but its component maybe large; order list. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "parent" array values for non-visited nodes; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "edges" list storing tuples and then iterating; okay. Could use arrays for speed but fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "sys.setrecursionlimit" not needed. Could include but no recursion.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))" for 4e5 ints, memory ~ 11MB, okay. Edges list also stores ints; data list remains if not freed. To save memory, we can parse and then `del data` after building edges? We can avoid data list by reading line by line. But N=2e5, data list 400k ints ~ 11MB, edges list tuples ~ 11MB, g_adj ~ maybe 20MB, total okay likely <256MB? Let's estimate more carefully.

Python memory:
- data list of 400001 Python ints: each int object 28 bytes, list pointers 8 bytes each => ~14.4MB? Actually int objects from map are created, list holds references; 400k*28=11.2MB + 400k*8=3.2MB =14.4MB.
- edges list of 199999 tuples: each tuple 56 bytes + references to ints (ints already from data? Wait when we do a=data[idx], b=data[idx+1], a and b are references to int objects in data list. Tuple stores references to those int objects, not copies. So tuple size 56 bytes + list pointer 8 = ~64*200k=12.8MB. The int objects are shared with data list. If we delete data after building edges, the int objects referenced by tuples remain; ints for N maybe. Actually a and b are int objects from data; tuples hold references, so they stay. data list deletion frees list pointers but not int objects referenced by tuples. Many int objects are referenced by tuples (two per edge), so 400k int objects remain ~11.2MB. Tuples ~11.2MB + list pointers 1.6MB. So edges ~24MB.
- deg list of N+1 ints: initially zeros all reference same 0? After increments, each element becomes int object; list pointers 1.6MB + int objects for degrees maybe many distinct but small ints cached? Small ints -5..256 are cached, degrees up to 2e5 not cached; each assignment creates int object? deg[a] +=1 creates new int, so up to N entries maybe 2e5 int objects ~5.6MB + list 1.6MB =7.2MB.
- good list of bool refs ~1.6MB.
- g_adj list of N+1 empty lists: 200001*56 =11.2MB + outer list 1.6MB =12.8MB. Edges appended as int references to existing int objects? When append b, it stores reference to int object b from tuple? Actually b is int object from tuple, so no new int. The list slots in inner lists: 2*(N-1) pointers ~3.2MB. Inner lists overallocate maybe. So g_adj ~16MB.
- visited, parent, f, g lists: each list of N+1 ints/bools. visited bool refs ~1.6MB. parent list initially zeros then assigned ints (some int objects from stack? parent[v]=u stores reference to int object u? u from stack/list, int object maybe from range? Actually range creates ints, stored in stack? Python ints for vertex ids from input data? When we iterate range, start is int object; parent[v]=u stores reference to that int object. Many parent values are references to existing int objects? Could be. List pointers 1.6MB each. f and g initially zeros, then assigned ints (new int objects) for good vertices; up to 2e5 each ~5.6MB +1.6 =7.2MB each. So arrays ~25MB.
- order list up to component size: pointers 1.6MB plus int refs (from stack) no new? stack stores ints from g_adj, references. order stores references. ~1.6MB.
Total maybe 14.4 data if not deleted + edges24 + deg7 + good1.6 + g_adj16 + visited1.6 + parent1.6 + f7 + g7 + order1.6 =82MB. Plus overhead. Under 256MB likely. But we can reduce by deleting data after building edges: `del data` after edges built. Then int objects referenced by tuples remain, but data list freed. Saves ~3.2MB list pointers. Good.

Alternatively, read line by line to avoid data list. Simpler and memory lower. Use sys.stdin.buffer.readline. N=2e5, line reading fine. Let's implement line by line to reduce memory. Code:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    ...

This creates int objects for a,b, stored in tuple. Fine. No data list. Good.

Now, memory of edges tuples still. Could store edges as two arrays from `array` module? Not necessary. But we can store edges as list of tuples; okay.

If memory limit is 256MB, fine. If 64MB, maybe tight. AtCoder typical 1024MB or 512MB. But let's consider optimizing further: We can avoid storing edges list by first reading all edges and degrees, but need edges later to build g_adj. We can store edges in two lists of ints: A=[0]*(N-1), B=[0]*(N-1) to avoid tuple overhead? Two lists of ints: list pointers 1.6MB each + int objects? When assign a from map, int object stored in A; B stores b. That's 400k int objects 11.2MB + list pointers 3.2MB =14.4MB, similar to tuples but less tuple overhead. Actually tuples add 56 bytes per edge ~11.2MB plus list pointers 1.6 =12.8, plus int objects 11.2 =24. Two lists: int objects 11.2 + list pointers 3.2 =14.4. So two lists saves ~10MB. Let's use two lists A and B for memory.

Code:
A = [0]*(N-1)
B = [0]*(N-1)
for i in range(N-1):
    a,b = map(int, input().split())
    A[i]=a; B[i]=b
    deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
Then later for i in range(N-1):
    a=A[i]; b=B[i]
    if good[a] and good[b]: ...
This is better. We'll do that.

Now, deg list: We can compute deg using A,B. Good.

Now, good list: bool.

Now, g_adj: list of lists. Could we avoid g_adj and process original edges with good to build adjacency? Need tree traversal on G. We can build g_adj. Fine.

Could we store g_adj as arrays of lists only for good vertices? We can use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` okay. If memory tight, we could use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` still. Good.

Now, visited, parent, f, g arrays. We can maybe use arrays of int from `array` to save memory, but not necessary.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `parent` array initialized to 0 and `visited` bool. Good.

Now, let's consider if we can compute DP without `g` array storing all g? We only need ans max and f for parent. g[u] not needed after ans update. But we store g list for simplicity. Could just compute `gu = 1 + s4; ans = max(ans, gu)` and not store g. We don't need g array. Let's remove g array to save memory. We only need f for children.
So:
f = [0]*(N+1)
...
gu = 1 + s4
if gu > ans: ans = gu
No g list. Good.

Do we need f for all nodes? Yes for parent. Store f.

Now, parent array needed for traversal and child identification. Could avoid parent array by storing children lists? Parent array fine.

Visited array needed. Good.

Now, order list per component. Good.

Now, let's think about if we can avoid parent array in postorder by using stack with state and computing f on exit; but parent array okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `parent[v] == u` in postorder if parent array for a neighbor that is not child but parent? For u, its parent p has parent[p] maybe not u, so skip. For children, parent[child]=u. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `visited` for good vertices; if a good vertex is in G component, visited true after DFS. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "any_good" if good exists but g_adj component maybe not connected? G is induced subgraph of a tree, so each component is a tree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "degree original" for vertices with degree >=4 but if N=2e5, deg list values up to 2e5. Good.

A = [0]*(N-1)
B = [0]*(N-1)
for i in range(N-1):
    a,b = map(int, input().split())
    A[i]=a; B[i]=b
    deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
Then later for i in range(N-1):
    a=A[i]; b=B[i]
    if good[a] and good[b]: ...
This is better. We'll do that.

Now, deg list: We can compute deg using A,B. Good.

Now, good list: bool.

Now, g_adj: list of lists. Could we avoid g_adj and process original edges with good to build adjacency? Need tree traversal on G. We can build g_adj. Fine.

Could we store g_adj as arrays of lists only for good vertices? We can use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` okay. If memory tight, we could use `g_adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]` still. Good.

Now, visited, parent, f, g arrays. We can maybe use arrays of int from `array` to save memory, but not necessary.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `parent` array initialized to 0 and `visited` bool. Good.

Now, let's consider if we can compute DP without `g` array storing all g? We only need ans max and f for parent. g[u] not needed after ans update. But we store g list for simplicity. Could just compute `gu = 1 + s4; ans = max(ans, gu)` and not store g. We don't need g array. Let's remove g array to save memory. We only need f for children.
So:
f = [0]*(N+1)
...
gu = 1 + s4
if gu > ans: ans = gu
No g list. Good.

Do we need f for all nodes? Yes for parent. Store f.

Now, parent array needed for traversal and child identification. Could avoid parent array by storing children lists? Parent array fine.

Visited array needed. Good.

Now, order list per component. Good.

Now, let's think about if we can avoid parent array in postorder by using stack with state and computing f on exit; but parent array okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `parent[v] == u` in postorder if parent array for a neighbor that is not child but parent? For u, its parent p has parent[p] maybe not u, so skip. For children, parent[child]=u. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with using `visited` for good vertices; if a good vertex is in G component, visited true after DFS. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "any_good" if good exists but g_adj component maybe not connected? G is induced subgraph of a tree, so each component is a tree. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "degree original" for vertices with degree >=4 but if N=2e5, deg list values up to 2e5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = 3*ans + 2" if ans is Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(-1)" if no good. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of an alkane with core size k where some core vertex has original degree >=4 but deg_S(v)=4 and original degree exactly4, so no leaves. If S has k>1, every vertex in S has at least one S neighbor. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subgraph" if we choose leaves that are not good and have original degree1; they are leaves. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "degree 1 or 4" for a core vertex with deg_S=4 and l=0; degree4. For a core vertex with deg_S=3 and l=1; degree4. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "at least one vertex of degree4" if S nonempty; all S vertices degree4. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possibility of a valid alkane where a vertex of degree4 has a neighbor of degree4 that is not in S? Contradiction: if neighbor degree4 in alkane, it is in S. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "tree T" and "subgraph" if the subgraph's vertex set includes a vertex of degree0? No, connected tree with >1 vertices no degree0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and deg[1]=0, good false, print -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=2" edge, deg both1, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=4" path, no good, -1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=5" star, good center, ans1, output5. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=6" tree with two centers? Need N=6: center1 deg3, center2 deg3? Not good. N=7 two centers each deg3? Not good. N=8 two centers each deg4 connected, output8. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sample 3" output 11; our DP ans3. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "maximum number of vertices" if there are multiple G components and one component has ans_core larger; max over all. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "rooting each component" if we start at a good vertex but its component maybe large; order list. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "parent" array values for non-visited nodes; no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "edges" list storing tuples and then iterating; okay. Could use arrays for speed but fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "sys.setrecursionlimit" not needed. Could include but no recursion.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))" for 4e5 ints, memory ~ 11MB, okay. Edges list also stores ints; data list remains if not freed. To save memory, we can parse and then `del data` after building edges? We can avoid data list by reading line by line. But N=2e5, data list 400k ints ~ 11MB, edges list tuples ~ 11MB, g_adj ~ maybe 20MB, total okay likely <256MB? Let's estimate more carefully.

Python memory:
- data list of 400001 Python ints: each int object 28 bytes, list pointers 8 bytes each => ~14.4MB? Actually int objects from map are created, list holds references; 400k*28=11.2MB + 400k*8=3.2MB =14.4MB.
- edges list of 199999 tuples: each tuple 56 bytes + references to ints (ints already from data? Wait when we do a=data[idx], b=data[idx+1], a and b are references to int objects in data list. Tuple stores references to those int objects, not copies. So tuple size 56 bytes + list pointer 8 = ~64*200k=12.8MB. The int objects are shared with data list. If we delete data after building edges, the int objects referenced by tuples remain; ints for N maybe. Actually a and b are int objects from data; tuples hold references, so they stay. data list deletion frees list pointers but not int objects referenced by tuples. Many int objects are referenced by tuples (two per edge), so 400k int objects remain ~11.2MB. Tuples ~11.2MB + list pointers 1.6MB. So edges ~24MB.
- deg list of N+1 ints: initially zeros all reference same 0? After increments, each element becomes int object; list pointers 1.6MB + int objects for degrees maybe many distinct but small ints cached? Small ints -5..256 are cached, degrees up to 2e5 not cached; each assignment creates int object? deg[a] +=1 creates new int, so up to N entries maybe 2e5 int objects ~5.6MB + list 1.6MB =7.2MB.
- good list of bool refs ~1.6MB.
- g_adj list of N+1 empty lists: 200001*56 =11.2MB + outer list 1.6MB =12.8MB. Edges appended as int references to existing int objects? When append b, it stores reference to int object b from tuple? Actually b is int object from tuple, so no new int. The list slots in inner lists: 2*(N-1) pointers ~3.2MB. Inner lists overallocate maybe. So g_adj ~16MB.
- visited, parent, f, g lists: each list of N+1 ints/bools. visited bool refs ~1.6MB. parent list initially zeros then assigned ints (some int objects from stack? parent[v]=u stores reference to int object u? u from stack/list, int object maybe from range? Actually range creates ints, stored in stack? Python ints for vertex ids from input data? When we iterate range, start is int object; parent[v]=u stores reference to that int object. Many parent values are references to existing int objects? Could be. List pointers 1.6MB each. f and g initially zeros, then assigned ints (new int objects) for good vertices; up to 2e5 each ~5.6MB +1.6 =7.2MB each. So arrays ~25MB.
- order list up to component size: pointers 1.6MB plus int refs (from stack) no new? stack stores ints from g_adj, references. order stores references. ~1.6MB.
Total maybe 14.4 data if not deleted + edges24 + deg7 + good1.6 + g_adj16 + visited1.6 + parent1.6 + f7 + g7 + order1.6 =82MB. Plus overhead. Under 256MB likely. But we can reduce by deleting data after building edges: `del data` after edges built. Then int objects referenced by tuples remain, but data list freed. Saves ~3.2MB list pointers. Good.

Alternatively, read line by line to avoid data list. Simpler and memory lower. Use sys.stdin.buffer.readline. N=2e5, line reading fine. Let's implement line by line to reduce memory. Code:

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N = int(input())
    edges = []
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a,b = map(int, input().split())
        edges.append((a,b))
        deg[a]+=1; deg[b]+=1
    ...

This creates int objects for a,b, stored in tuple. Fine. No data list. Good.

Now, memory of edges tuples still. Could store edges as two arrays from `array` module? Not necessary. But we can store edges as list of tuples; okay.

If memory limit is 256MB, fine. If 64MB, maybe tight. AtCoder typical 1024MB or 512MB. But let's consider optimizing further: We can avoid storing edges list by first reading all edges and degrees, but need edges later to build g_adj. We can store edges in two lists of ints: A=[0]*(N-1), B=[0]*(N-1) to avoid tuple overhead? Two lists of ints: list pointers 1.6MB each + int objects? When assign a from map, int object stored in A; B stores b. That's 400k int objects 11.2MB + list pointers 3.2MB =14.4MB, similar to tuples but less tuple overhead. Actually tuples add 56 bytes per edge ~11.2MB plus list pointers 1.6 =12.8, plus int objects 11.2 =24. Two lists: int objects 11.2 + list pointers 3.2 =14.4. So two lists saves ~10MB. Let's use two lists A and B for memory.
